double distanceTolerance) {\n    66→\t\tfinal KDTree<PVector> tree = KDTree.create(2);\n    67→\t\tfinal List<PVector> newPoints = new ArrayList<>();\n    68→\t\tfor (PVector p : points) {\n    69→\t\t\tfinal double[] coords = new double[] { p.x, p.y };\n    70→\t\t\tif (tree.size() == 0 || tree.query1NN(coords).dist() > distanceTolerance) {\n    71→\t\t\t\ttree.insert(coords, p);\n    72→\t\t\t\tnewPoints.add(p);\n    73→\t\t\t}\n    74→\t\t}\n    75→\t\treturn newPoints;\n    76→\t}\n    77→\n    78→\t/**\n    79→\t * Sorts a list of points according to the Hilbert space-filling curve to ensure\n    80→\t * a high-degree of spatial locality in the sequence of points.\n    81→\t * \n    82→\t * @param points list of points to sort. a list requires at least 24 points to\n    83→\t *               be sorted.\n    84→\t * @return a sorted <b>copy</b> of the input list, having a different order\n    85→\t *         according to points' Hilbert ranking of their (x, y) coordinate\n    86→\t * @since 1.3.0\n    87→\t */\n    88→\tpublic static List<PVector> hilbertSort(List<PVector> points) {\n    89→\t\tdouble xMin, xMax, yMin, yMax;\n    90→\t\tif (points.isEmpty()) {\n    91→\t\t\treturn points;\n    92→\t\t}\n    93→\n    94→\t\t// find bounds\n    95→\t\tPVector v = points.get(0);\n    96→\t\txMin = v.x;\n    97→\t\txMax = v.x;\n    98→\t\tyMin = v.y;\n    99→\t\tyMax = v.y;\n   100→\n   101→\t\tfor (PVector PVector : points) {\n   102→\t\t\tif (PVector.x < xMin) {\n   103→\t\t\t\txMin = PVector.x;\n   104→\t\t\t} else if (PVector.x > xMax) {\n   105→\t\t\t\txMax = PVector.x;\n   106→\t\t\t}\n   107→\t\t\tif (PVector.y < yMin) {\n   108→\t\t\t\tyMin = PVector.y;\n   109→\t\t\t} else if (PVector.y > yMax) {\n   110→\t\t\t\tyMax = PVector.y;\n   111→\t\t\t}\n   112→\t\t}\n   113→\n   114→\t\tdouble xDelta = xMax - xMin;\n   115→\t\tdouble yDelta = yMax - yMin;\n   116→\t\tif (xDelta == 0 || yDelta == 0) {\n   117→\t\t\treturn points;\n   118→\t\t}\n   119→\t\tif (points.size() < 24) {\n   120→\t\t\treturn points;\n   121→\t\t}\n   122→\n   123→\t\tdouble hn = Math.log(points.size()) / 0.693147180559945 / 2.0;\n   124→\t\tint nHilbert = (int) Math.floor(hn + 0.5);\n   125→\t\tif (nHilbert < 4) {\n   126→\t\t\tnHilbert = 4;\n   127→\t\t}\n   128→\n   129→\t\t// could also use SortedMap<index -> point>\n   130→\t\tList<Pair<Integer, PVector>> ranks = new ArrayList<>(points.size());\n   131→\t\tdouble hScale = (1 << nHilbert) - 1.0;\n   132→\t\t// scale coordinates to 2^n - 1\n   133→\t\tfor (PVector vh : points) {\n   134→\t\t\tint ix = (int) (hScale * (vh.x - xMin) / xDelta);\n   135→\t\t\tint iy = (int) (hScale * (vh.y - yMin) / yDelta);\n   136→\t\t\tranks.add(new Pair<>(xy2Hilbert(ix, iy, nHilbert), vh));\n   137→\t\t}\n   138→\n   139→\t\tranks.sort((a, b) -> Integer.compare(a.getFirst(), b.getFirst()));\n   140→\n   141→\t\treturn ranks.stream().map(Pair::getSecond).collect(Collectors.toList());\n   142→\t}\n   143→\n   144→\t/**\n   145→\t * Clusters points into N groups, using k-means clustering.\n   146→\t * <p>\n   147→\t * K-means finds the N cluster centers and assigns points to the nearest cluster\n   148→\t * center, such that the squared (euclidean) distances from the cluster are\n   149→\t * minimised.\n   150→\t * \n   151→\t * @param points list of points to cluster\n   152→\t * @param groups desired number of clustered groups\n   153→\t * @since 1.3.1\n   154→\t * @see #cluster(Collection, int, long)\n   155→\t * @return list of groups, where each group is a list of PVectors\n   156→\t */\n   157→\tpublic static List<List<PVector>> cluster(Collection<PVector> points, int groups) {\n   158→\t\treturn cluster(points, groups, System.nanoTime());\n   159→\t}\n   160→\n   161→\t/**\n   162→\t * Clusters points into N groups, using k-means clustering.\n   163→\t * <p>\n   164→\t * K-means finds the N cluster centers and assigns points to the nearest cluster\n   165→\t * center, such that the squared (euclidean) distances from the cluster are\n   166→\t * minimised.\n   167→\t * \n   168→\t * @param points list of points to cluster\n   169→\t * @param groups desired number of clustered groups\n   170→\t * @param seed   random seed\n   171→\t * @since 1.3.1\n   172→\t * @return list of groups, where each group is a list of PVectors\n   173→\t * @see #cluster(Collection, int)\n   174→\t */\n   175→\tpublic static List<List<PVector>> cluster(Collection<PVector> points, int groups, long seed) {\n   176→\t\tRandomGenerator r = new XoRoShiRo128PlusRandomGenerator(seed);\n   177→\t\tClusterer<CPVector> kmeans = new KMeansPlusPlusClusterer<>(groups, 25, new EuclideanDistance(), r);\n   178→\t\tList<CPVector> pointz = points.stream().map(p -> new CPVector(p)).collect(Collectors.toList());\n   179→\n   180→\t\tList<List<PVector>> clusters = new ArrayList<>(groups);\n   181→\t\tkmeans.cluster(pointz).forEach(cluster -> {\n   182→\t\t\tclusters.add(cluster.getPoints().stream().map(p -> p.p).collect(Collectors.toList()));\n   183→\t\t});\n   184→\n   185→\t\treturn clusters;\n   186→\t}\n   187→\n   188→\t/**\n   189→\t * Finds the geometric median point of a set of weighted sample points.\n   190→\t * <p>\n   191→\t * The median point is the point that minimises the sum of (weighted) distances\n   192→\t * to the sample points.\n   193→\t * <p>\n   194→\t * Points are expressed as PVectors; the z coordinate is used as the weight for\n   195→\t * each point. Weights must be positive. If every point has a weight of 0 (z=0),\n   196→\t * the function returns the median as if each point had an equal non-zero weight\n   197→\t * (set to 1).\n   198→\t * \n   199→\t * @param points list of points, where the z coordinate is point weight\n   200→\t * @since 1.3.1\n   201→\t * @return 2D median point\n   202→\t */\n   203→\tpublic static PVector weightedMedian(Collection<PVector> points) {\n   204→\t\tboolean allZero = points.stream().allMatch(p -> p.z == 0);\n   205→\t\tPoint4d[] wp = points.stream().map(p -> new Point4d(p.x, p.y, 0, allZero ? 1 : p.z)).toArray(Point4d[]::new);\n   206→\t\tPoint3d median = GeometricMedian.median(wp, 1e-3, 50);\n   207→\t\treturn new PVector((float) median.x, (float) median.y);\n   208→\t}\n   209→\n   210→\t/**\n   211→\t * Generates a set of random (uniform) points that lie within a bounding\n   212→\t * rectangle.\n   213→\t * \n   214→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   215→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   216→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   217→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   218→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   219→\t * @return\n   220→\t * @see #random(double, double, double, double, int, long) seeded random()\n   221→\t */\n   222→\tpublic static List<PVector> random(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n   223→\t\treturn random(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, n, System.nanoTime());\n   224→\t}\n   225→\n   226→\t/**\n   227→\t * Generates a set of random (uniform) points that lie within a bounding\n   228→\t * rectangle, using the specified seed.\n   229→\t * \n   230→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   231→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   232→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   233→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   234→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   235→\t * @param seed number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n   236→\t *             generator\n   237→\t * @return\n   238→\t * @see #random(double, double, double, double, int) non-seeded random()\n   239→\t */\n   240→\tpublic static List<PVector> random(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n, long seed) {\n   241→\t\tfinal SplittableRandom random = new SplittableRandom(seed);\n   242→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   243→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n   244→\t\t\tfinal float x = (float) (xMin + (xMax - xMin) * random.nextDouble());\n   245→\t\t\tfinal float y = (float) (yMin + (yMax - yMin) * random.nextDouble());\n   246→\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x, y));\n   247→\t\t}\n   248→\t\treturn points;\n   249→\t}\n   250→\n   251→\t/**\n   252→\t * Generates a set of random points having a gaussian/normal distribution. The\n   253→\t * point set is centered around the given center, given by mean coordinates.\n   254→\t * \n   255→\t * @param centerX x coordinate of the center/mean of the point set\n   256→\t * @param centerY x coordinate of the center/mean of the point set\n   257→\t * @param sd      standard deviation, which specifies how much the values can\n   258→\t *                vary from the mean. 68% of point samples have a value within\n   259→\t *                one standard deviation of the mean; three standard deviations\n   260→\t *                account for 99.7% of the sample population\n   261→\t * @param n       number of points to generate\n   262→\t * @return\n   263→\t * @see #gaussian(double, double, double, int, long) seeded gaussian()\n   264→\t */\n   265→\tpublic static List<PVector> gaussian(double centerX, double centerY, double sd, int n) {\n   266→\t\treturn gaussian(centerX, centerY, sd, n, System.nanoTime());\n   267→\t}\n   268→\n   269→\t/**\n   270→\t * Generates a set of random points having a gaussian/normal distribution, using\n   271→\t * the specified seed. The point set is centered around the given center, given\n   272→\t * by mean coordinates.\n   273→\t * \n   274→\t * @param centerX x coordinate of the center/mean of the point set\n   275→\t * @param centerY x coordinate of the center/mean of the point set\n   276→\t * @param sd      standard deviation, which specifies how much the values can\n   277→\t *                vary from the mean. 68% of point samples have a value within\n   278→\t *                one standard deviation of the mean; three standard deviations\n   279→\t *                account for 99.7% of the sample population\n   280→\t * @param n       number of points to generate\n   281→\t * @param seed    number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n   282→\t *                generator\n   283→\t * @return\n   284→\t * @see #gaussian(double, double, double, int) non-seeded gaussian()\n   285→\t */\n   286→\tpublic static List<PVector> gaussian(double centerX, double centerY, double sd, int n, long seed) {\n   287→\t\tfinal RandomGenerator random = new XoRoShiRo128PlusRandomGenerator(seed);\n   288→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   289→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n   290→\t\t\tfinal float x = (float) (sd * random.nextGaussian() + centerX);\n   291→\t\t\tfinal float y = (float) (sd * random.nextGaussian() + centerY);\n   292→\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x, y));\n   293→\t\t}\n   294→\t\treturn points;\n   295→\t}\n   296→\n   297→\t/**\n   298→\t * Generates a square grid/lattice of points that lie within a bounding\n   299→\t * rectangle.\n   300→\t * \n   301→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   302→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   303→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   304→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   305→\t * @return\n   306→\t */\n   307→\tpublic static List<PVector> squareGrid(final double xMin, final double yMin, final double xMax, final double yMax,\n   308→\t\t\tfinal double pointDistance) {\n   309→\t\tfinal double width = xMax - xMin;\n   310→\t\tfinal double height = yMax - yMin;\n   311→\n   312→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>();\n   313→\n   314→\t\tfor (double x = 0; x < width; x += pointDistance) {\n   315→\t\t\tfor (double y = 0; y < height; y += pointDistance) {\n   316→\t\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector((float) (x + xMin), (float) (y + yMin)));\n   317→\t\t\t}\n   318→\t\t}\n   319→\t\treturn points;\n   320→\t}\n   321→\n   322→\t/**\n   323→\t * Generates a hexagon grid/lattice of points that lie within a bounding\n   324→\t * rectangle.\n   325→\t * \n   326→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   327→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   328→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   329→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   330→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   331→\t * @return\n   332→\t * @see #hexGrid(double, double, double, double, double) hexGrid() where\n   333→\t *      inter-point distance is specified\n   334→\t */\n   335→\tpublic static List<PVector> hexGrid(final double xMin, final double yMin, final double xMax, final double yMax, final int n) {\n   336→\t\tfinal double width = xMax - xMin;\n   337→\t\tfinal double height = yMax - yMin;\n   338→\n   339→\t\tfinal float h = (float) Math.sqrt((width * height * (Math.sqrt(5) / 2)) / n);\n   340→\t\tfinal float v = (float) (h * (2 / Math.sqrt(5)));\n   341→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   342→\n   343→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < width / h; i++) {\n   344→\t\t\tfor (int j = 0; j < height / v; j++) {\n   345→\t\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector((i - (j % 2) / 2f) * h + (float) xMin, j * v + (float) yMin));\n   346→\t\t\t}\n   347→\t\t}\n   348→\t\treturn points;\n   349→\t}\n   350→\n   351→\t/**\n   352→\t * Generates a hexagon grid of points that lie within a bounding rectangle.\n   353→\t * \n   354→\t * @param xMin          x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   355→\t * @param yMin          y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   356→\t * @param xMax          x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   357→\t * @param yMax          y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   358→\t * @param pointDistance inter-point distance\n   359→\t * @return\n   360→\t * @see #hexGrid(double, double, double, double, int) hexGrid() where number of\n   361→\t *      points is specified\n   362→\t */\n   363→\tpublic static List<PVector> hexGrid(final double xMin, final double yMin, final double xMax, final double yMax,\n   364→\t\t\tfinal double pointDistance) {\n   365→\t\tfinal double width = xMax - xMin;\n   366→\t\tfinal double height = yMax - yMin;\n   367→\n   368→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>();\n   369→\n   370→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < width / pointDistance; i++) {\n   371→\t\t\tfor (int j = 0; j < height / pointDistance; j++) {\n   372→\t\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector((float) ((i - (j % 2) / 2f) * pointDistance + xMin), (float) (j * pointDistance + yMin)));\n   373→\t\t\t}\n   374→\t\t}\n   375→\t\treturn points;\n   376→\t}\n   377→\n   378→\t/**\n   379→\t * Generates a hexagonal grid of points <b>arranged in a hexagon pattern</b>.\n   380→\t * \n   381→\t * @param centerX x coordinate of the hexagon center point\n   382→\t * @param centerY y coordinate of the hexagon center point\n   383→\t * @param length  layers/no. of points on each hexagon side\n   384→\t * @distance inter-point distance\n   385→\t * @return\n   386→\t */\n   387→\tpublic static List<PVector> hexagon(double centerX, double centerY, int length, double distance) {\n   388→\t\tfinal float xOffset = (float) centerX;\n   389→\t\tfinal float yOffset = (float) centerY;\n   390→\t\tfinal float d = (float) distance;\n   391→\n   392→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>();\n   393→\n   394→\t\t/*\n   395→\t\t * PVector .z is set to length so hexagon layer can be easily identified.\n   396→\t\t */\n   397→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i <= (length - 1); i++) {\n   398→\t\t\tfloat y = (SQRT_3 * i * d) / 2.0f;\n   399→\t\t\tfor (int j = 0; j < (2 * length - 1 - i); j++) {\n   400→\t\t\t\tfloat x = (-(2 * length - i - 2) * d) / 2.0f + j * d;\n   401→\t\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x + xOffset, y + yOffset, length));\n   402→\t\t\t\tif (y != 0) {\n   403→\t\t\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x + xOffset, -y + yOffset, length));\n   404→\t\t\t\t}\n   405→\t\t\t}\n   406→\t\t}\n   407→\t\treturn points;\n   408→\t}\n   409→\n   410→\t/**\n   411→\t * Generates a set of n points that are randomly distributed on a ring\n   412→\t * (annulus).\n   413→\t * \n   414→\t * @param centerX     x coordinate of the center/mean of the ring\n   415→\t * @param centerY     x coordinate of the center/mean of the ring\n   416→\t * @param innerRadius radius of the ring's hole\n   417→\t * @param outerRadius outer radius of the ring\n   418→\t * @param maxAngle    angle of the ring (in radians). Can be negative\n   419→\t * @param n           the number of random points to generate\n   420→\t * @return a list of PVector objects representing the (x, y) coordinates of the\n   421→\t *         random points\n   422→\t * @see #ring(double, double, double, double, double, int, long) seeded ring()\n   423→\t */\n   424→\tpublic static List<PVector> ring(double centerX, double centerY, double innerRadius, double outerRadius, double maxAngle, int n) {\n   425→\t\treturn ring(centerX, centerY, innerRadius, outerRadius, maxAngle, n, System.nanoTime());\n   426→\t}\n   427→\n   428→\t/**\n   429→\t * Generates a set of points that are randomly distributed on a ring (annulus).\n   430→\t * \n   431→\t * @param centerX     x coordinate of the center/mean of the ring\n   432→\t * @param centerY     x coordinate of the center/mean of the ring\n   433→\t * @param innerRadius radius of the ring's hole\n   434→\t * @param outerRadius outer radius of the ring\n   435→\t * @param maxAngle    angle of the ring (in radians). Can be negative\n   436→\t * @param n           the number of random points to generate\n   437→\t * @param seed        number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom\n   438→\t *                    number generator\n   439→\t * @return a list of PVector objects representing the (x, y) coordinates of the\n   440→\t *         random points\n   441→\t * @see #ring(double, double, double, double, double, int) non-seeded ring()\n   442→\t */\n   443→\tpublic static List<PVector> ring(double centerX, double centerY, double innerRadius, double outerRadius, double maxAngle, int n,\n   444→\t\t\tlong seed) {\n   445→\t\tfinal SplittableRandom random = new SplittableRandom(seed);\n   446→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   447→\t\tif (maxAngle == 0) {\n   448→\t\t\tmaxAngle = Double.MIN_VALUE;\n   449→\t\t}\n   450→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n   451→\t\t\tdouble randomAngle = (maxAngle < 0 ? -1 : 1) * random.nextDouble(Math.abs(maxAngle));\n   452→\t\t\tdouble randomRadius = random.nextDouble(innerRadius, outerRadius);\n   453→\t\t\tdouble x = -Math.sin(randomAngle) * randomRadius;\n   454→\t\t\tdouble y = Math.cos(randomAngle) * randomRadius;\n   455→\n   456→\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector((float) (x + centerX), (float) (y + centerY)));\n   457→\t\t}\n   458→\t\treturn points;\n   459→\t}\n   460→\n   461→\t/**\n   462→\t * Generates a set of random points (constrained within a rectangular region)\n   463→\t * via Poisson Disk Sampling.\n   464→\t * <p>\n   465→\t * Poisson-disc sampling produces points that are tightly-packed, but no closer\n   466→\t * to each other than a specified minimum distance, resulting in a more natural\n   467→\t * and desirable pattern for many applications. This distribution is also\n   468→\t * described as blue noise.\n   469→\t * \n   470→\t * @param xMin    x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   471→\t * @param yMin    y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   472→\t * @param xMax    x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   473→\t * @param yMax    y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   474→\t * @param minDist minimum euclidean distance between any two points\n   475→\t * @return\n   476→\t * @see #poisson(double, double, double, double, double, long) seeded poisson()\n   477→\t */\n   478→\tpublic static List<PVector> poisson(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, double minDist) {\n   479→\t\treturn poisson(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, minDist, System.nanoTime());\n   480→\t}\n   481→\n   482→\t/**\n   483→\t * Generates a set of random points (constrained within a rectangular region)\n   484→\t * via Poisson Disk Sampling, using the specified seed.\n   485→\t * <p>\n   486→\t * Poisson-disc sampling produces points that are tightly-packed, but no closer\n   487→\t * to each other than a specified minimum distance, resulting in a more natural\n   488→\t * and desirable pattern for many applications. This distribution is also\n   489→\t * described as blue noise.\n   490→\t * \n   491→\t * @param xMin    x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   492→\t * @param yMin    y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   493→\t * @param xMax    x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   494→\t * @param yMax    y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   495→\t * @param minDist minimum euclidean distance between any two points\n   496→\t * @param seed    number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n   497→\t *                generator\n   498→\t * @return\n   499→\t * @see #poisson(double, double, double, double, double) non-seeded poisson()\n   500→\t */\n   501→\tpublic static List<PVector> poisson(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, double minDist, long seed) {\n   502→\t\tfinal PoissonDistributionJRUS pd = new PoissonDistributionJRUS(seed);\n   503→\t\treturn pd.generate(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, minDist);\n   504→\t}\n   505→\n   506→\t/**\n   507→\t * Generates a poisson point set having N points constrained within a\n   508→\t * rectangular region.\n   509→\t * <p>\n   510→\t * Poisson-disc sampling produces points that are tightly-packed, but no closer\n   511→\t * to each other than a specified minimum distance, resulting in a more natural\n   512→\t * and desirable pattern for many applications. This distribution is also\n   513→\t * described as blue noise.\n   514→\t * \n   515→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   516→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   517→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   518→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   519→\t * @param n    target size of poisson point set\n   520→\t * @param seed number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n   521→\t *             generator\n   522→\t * @return\n   523→\t */\n   524→\tpublic static List<PVector> poissonN(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n, long seed) {\n   525→\t\tfinal PoissonDistributionJRUS pd = new PoissonDistributionJRUS(seed);\n   526→\t\treturn pd.generate(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, n);\n   527→\t}\n   528→\n   529→\t/**\n   530→\t * Generates a set of points arranged in a phyllotaxis pattern (an arrangement\n   531→\t * similar to the florets in the head of a sunflower), using the golden ratio\n   532→\t * (the most irrational number) to position points with the least possible\n   533→\t * aliasing (which is arguably the \"best\" arrangement).\n   534→\t * \n   535→\t * @param centerX x coordinate of the center of the point set\n   536→\t * @param centerY y coordinate of the center of the point set\n   537→\t * @param n       number of points to generate\n   538→\t * @param radius  radius of circular phyllotaxis extent (max distance of a point\n   539→\t *                from the center position)\n   540→\t * @return\n   541→\t */\n   542→\tpublic static List<PVector> phyllotaxis(double centerX, double centerY, int n, double radius) {\n   543→\t\treturn phyllotaxis(centerX, centerY, n, radius, 2 * Math.PI - GOLDEN_ANGLE);\n   544→\t}\n   545→\n   546→\t/**\n   547→\t * Generates a set of points arranged in a phyllotaxis pattern (an arrangement\n   548→\t * similar to the florets in the head of a sunflower), using a user-defined\n   549→\t * theta.\n   550→\t * \n   551→\t * @param centerX x coordinate of the center of the point set\n   552→\t * @param centerY y coordinate of the center of the point set\n   553→\t * @param n       number of points to generate\n   554→\t * @param radius  radius of circular phyllotaxis extent (max distance of a point\n   555→\t *                from the center position)\n   556→\t * @param theta   angle (in radians) to turn after each point placement\n   557→\t * @return\n   558→\t */\n   559→\tpublic static List<PVector> phyllotaxis(double centerX, double centerY, int n, double radius, double theta) {\n   560→\t\tfinal double fillArea = radius * radius * Math.PI; // calculate area to be filled\n   561→\t\tfinal double circleSpace = (fillArea / n); // area per circle\n   562→\t\tfinal double fudge = 0.7; // Fudge factor: breathing space between circles\n   563→\t\tfinal float circleRadius = (float) (Math.sqrt(circleSpace / Math.PI) * fudge);\n   564→\n   565→\t\tfloat cumArea = 0; // cumulative circle area\n   566→\n   567→\t\tfinal List<PVector> outList = new ArrayList<>();\n   568→\t\tfor (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {\n   569→\t\t\tfinal double angle = i * theta; // rotation per circle\n   570→\t\t\tcumArea += circleSpace; // add sm_area to cum_area every loop\n   571→\n   572→\t\t\tfinal double spiralR = Math.sqrt(cumArea / Math.PI); // expansion of spiral (distance of circle) per loop\n   573→\n   574→\t\t\tfloat pX = (float) (centerX + Math.cos(angle) * spiralR); // spiral rotation of golden angle per loop on X\n   575→\t\t\tfloat pY = (float) (centerY + Math.sin(angle) * spiralR); // spiral rotation of golden angle per loop on Y\n   576→\n   577→\t\t\toutList.add(new PVector(pX, pY, circleRadius));\n   578→\t\t}\n   579→\t\treturn outList;\n   580→\t}\n   581→\n   582→\t/**\n   583→\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n   584→\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on an irrational number (the\n   585→\t * plastic constant).\n   586→\t * <p>\n   587→\t * The <i>plastic LDS</i> has been <a href=\n   588→\t * \"http://extremelearning.com.au/unreasonable-effectiveness-of-quasirandom-sequences/\">shown</a>\n   589→\t * to have superior low discrepancy properties amongst the quasirandom\n   590→\t * sequences, and is therefore recommended.\n   591→\t * <p>\n   592→\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n   593→\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n   594→\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n   595→\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n   596→\t * \n   597→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   598→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   599→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   600→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   601→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   602→\t * @return\n   603→\t */\n   604→\tpublic static List<PVector> plasticLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n   605→\t\t// https://github.com/Atrix256/SampleZoo/blob/master/src/families/_2d/samples/irrational_numbers/irrational_numbers.cpp\n   606→\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n   607→\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n   608→\t\tfinal double p = 1.32471795724474602596; // plastic constant\n   609→\t\tfinal double a1 = 1.0 / p; // inverse of plastic number\n   610→\t\tfinal double a2 = 1.0 / (p * p);\n   611→\n   612→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   613→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n   614→\t\t\tfinal float x = (float) (((0.5 + a1 * i) % 1) * w + xMin);\n   615→\t\t\tfinal float y = (float) (((0.5 + a2 * i) % 1) * h + yMin);\n   616→\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x, y));\n   617→\t\t}\n   618→\t\treturn points;\n   619→\t}\n   620→\n   621→\t/**\n   622→\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n   623→\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on an irrational number. In this\n   624→\t * method, a random jitter is added to points to give the point set\n   625→\t * blue-noise-like properties.\n   626→\t * <p>\n   627→\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n   628→\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n   629→\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n   630→\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n   631→\t * \n   632→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   633→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   634→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   635→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   636→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   637→\t * @return\n   638→\t * @see #plasticJitteredLDS(double, double, double, double, int, long) seeded\n   639→\t *      irrationalJitteredLDS()\n   640→\t */\n   641→\tpublic static List<PVector> plasticJitteredLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n   642→\t\treturn plasticJitteredLDS(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, n, System.nanoTime());\n   643→\t}\n   644→\n   645→\t/**\n   646→\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n   647→\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on an irrational number. In this\n   648→\t * method, a random jitter is added to points to give the point set\n   649→\t * blue-noise-like properties.\n   650→\t * <p>\n   651→\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n   652→\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n   653→\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n   654→\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n   655→\t * \n   656→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   657→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   658→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   659→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   660→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   661→\t * @param seed number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n   662→\t *             generator\n   663→\t * @return\n   664→\t * @see #plasticJitteredLDS(double, double, double, double, int) non-seeded\n   665→\t *      irrationalJitteredLDS()\n   666→\t */\n   667→\tpublic static List<PVector> plasticJitteredLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n, long seed) {\n   668→\t\t// https://github.com/Atrix256/SampleZoo/blob/master/src/families/_2d/samples/irrational_numbers/irrational_numbers.cpp\n   669→\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n   670→\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n   671→\n   672→\t\tfinal SplittableRandom random = new SplittableRandom(seed);\n   673→\t\tfinal double p = 1.32471795724474602596; // plastic constant\n   674→\t\tfinal double a1 = 1.0 / p;\n   675→\t\tfinal double a2 = 1.0 / (p * p);\n   676→\t\tfinal double c_magicNumber = 0.732;\n   677→\n   678→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   679→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n   680→\t\t\tfinal float x = (float) (((random.nextDouble() * c_magicNumber / Math.sqrt(i + 1d) + a1 * i) % 1) * w + xMin);\n   681→\t\t\tfinal float y = (float) (((random.nextDouble() * c_magicNumber / Math.sqrt(i + 1d) + a2 * i) % 1) * h + yMin);\n   682→\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x, y));\n   683→\t\t}\n   684→\t\treturn points;\n   685→\t}\n   686→\n   687→\t/**\n   688→\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n   689→\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on a Halton sequence.\n   690→\t * <p>\n   691→\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n   692→\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n   693→\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n   694→\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n   695→\t * \n   696→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   697→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   698→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   699→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   700→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   701→\t * @return\n   702→\t */\n   703→\tpublic static List<PVector> haltonLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n   704→\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n   705→\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n   706→\t\tfloat[][] values = new float[n][2];\n   707→\t\tvanDerCorput(values, 2, 0, true, 0);\n   708→\t\tvanDerCorput(values, 3, 1, true, 0);\n   709→\n   710→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   711→\t\tfor (float[] point : values) {\n   712→\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector((float) (point[0] * w + xMin), (float) (point[1] * h + yMin)));\n   713→\t\t}\n   714→\t\treturn points;\n   715→\t}\n   716→\n   717→\t/**\n   718→\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n   719→\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on a Hammersley sequence.\n   720→\t * <p>\n   721→\t * The Hammersley sequence in 2D is just the 1d Van Der Corput sequence on one\n   722→\t * axis, and regular sampling on the other axis.\n   723→\t * <p>\n   724→\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n   725→\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n   726→\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n   727→\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n   728→\t * \n   729→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   730→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   731→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   732→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   733→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   734→\t * @return\n   735→\t */\n   736→\tpublic static List<PVector> hammersleyLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n   737→\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n   738→\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n   739→\n   740→\t\tfloat[][] values = new float[n][2];\n   741→\t\tvanDerCorput(values, 2, 0, false, 0);\n   742→\n   743→\t\tfinal float offset = 1.0f / (n * 2);\n   744→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n   745→\t\t\tvalues[i][1] = offset + (i / (float) n);\n   746→\t\t}\n   747→\n   748→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   749→\t\tfor (float[] point : values) {\n   750→\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector((float) (point[0] * w + xMin), (float) (point[1] * h + yMin)));\n   751→\t\t}\n   752→\t\treturn points;\n   753→\t}\n   754→\n   755→\t/**\n   756→\t * Generates a set of random stratified points (bounded by a rectangle) based on\n   757→\t * the \"N-Rooks\" sampling pattern.\n   758→\t * <p>\n   759→\t * N-Rooks is a sampling pattern where you treat the boundary as if it were a\n   760→\t * chess board. Every sampling position is a rook that could move horizontally\n   761→\t * or vertically, and should be placed such that none of these rooks could\n   762→\t * capture/\"see\" any of the other rooks. In other words, every column has a\n   763→\t * single sample point in it, and every row has a single sample point in it.\n   764→\t * \n   765→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   766→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   767→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   768→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   769→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   770→\t * @return\n   771→\t * @see #nRooksLDS(double, double, double, double, int, long)\n   772→\t */\n   773→\tpublic static List<PVector> nRooksLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n   774→\t\treturn nRooksLDS(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, n, System.nanoTime());\n   775→\t}\n   776→\n   777→\t/**\n   778→\t * Generates a set of random stratified points (bounded by a rectangle) based on\n   779→\t * the \"N-Rooks\" sampling pattern.\n   780→\t * <p>\n   781→\t * N-Rooks is a sampling pattern where you treat the boundary as if it were a\n   782→\t * chess board. Every sampling position is a rook that could move horizontally\n   783→\t * or vertically, and should be placed such that none of these rooks could\n   784→\t * capture/\"see\" any of the other rooks. In other words, every column has a\n   785→\t * single sample point in it, and every row has a single sample point in it.\n   786→\t * \n   787→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   788→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   789→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   790→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   791→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   792→\t * @param seed number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n   793→\t *             generator\n   794→\t * @return\n   795→\t * @see #nRooksLDS(double, double, double, double, int)\n   796→\t */\n   797→\tpublic static List<PVector> nRooksLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n, long seed) {\n   798→\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n   799→\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n   800→\n   801→\t\tfinal List<Integer> rookPositions = IntStream.range(0, n).boxed().collect(Collectors.toList());\n   802→\t\tCollections.shuffle(rookPositions, new XoRoShiRo128PlusRandom(seed));\n   803→\n   804→\t\tfinal float offset = 1.0f / (n * 2);\n   805→\n   806→\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   807→\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n   808→\t\t\tfloat x = offset + (rookPositions.get(i) / (float) n);\n   809→\t\t\tx *= w;\n   810→\t\t\tx += xMin;\n   811→\t\t\tfloat y = offset + i / (float) n;\n   812→\t\t\ty *= h;\n   813→\t\t\ty += yMin;\n   814→\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x, y));\n   815→\t\t}\n   816→\n   817→\t\treturn points;\n   818→\t}\n   819→\n   820→\t/**\n   821→\t * Generates a 2D set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a\n   822→\t * rectangle) from the Sobol low discrepancy sequence (LDS).\n   823→\t * <p>\n   824→\t * A Sobol sequence is a low-discrepancy sequence with the property that for all\n   825→\t * values of N,its subsequence (x1, ... xN) has a low discrepancy. It can be\n   826→\t * used to generate pseudo-randompoints in a space S, which are\n   827→\t * equi-distributed.\n   828→\t * \n   829→\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   830→\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n   831→\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   832→\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n   833→\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n   834→\t * @since 1.3.1\n   835→\t * @return\n   836→\t */\n   837→\tpublic static List<PVector> sobolLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n   838→\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n   839→\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n   840→\t\tfinal int dimension = 2;\n   841→\t\tfinal int BITS = 52;\n   842→\t\tfinal double SCALE = FastMath.pow(2, BITS);\n   843→\t\tfinal long[][] direction = new long[dimension][BITS + 1];\n   844→\t\tfinal long[] x = new long[dimension];\n   845→\t\tfinal int[] m = new int[] { 0, 1 };\n   846→\t\tfinal int a = 0;\n   847→\t\tfinal int s = m.length - 1;\n   848→\n   849→\t\tfor (int i = 1; i <= BITS; i++) {\n   850→\t\t\tdirection[0][i] = 1l << (BITS - i);\n   851→\t\t}\n   852→\n   853→\t\t// init direction vector\n   854→\t\tfinal int d = 1;\n   855→\t\tfor (int i = 1; i <= s; i++) {\n   856→\t\t\tdirection[d][i] = ((long) m[i]) << (BITS - i);\n   857→\t\t}\n   858→\t\tfor (int i = s + 1; i <= BITS; i++) {\n   859→\t\t\tdirection[d][i] = direction[d][i - s] ^ (direction[d][i - s] >> s);\n   860→\t\t\tfor (int k = 1; k <= s - 1; k++) {\n   861→\t\t\t\tdirection[d][i] ^= ((a >> (s - 1 - k)) & 1) * direction[d][i - k];\n   862→\t\t\t}\n   863→\t\t}\n   864→\n   865→\t\tList<PVector> output = new ArrayList<>(n);\n   866→\t\tfor (int i = 1; i < n; i++) {\n   867→\n   868→\t\t\t// find the index c of the rightmost 0\n   869→\t\t\tint c = 1;\n   870→\t\t\tint value = i - 1;\n   871→\t\t\twhile ((value & 1) == 1) {\n   872→\t\t\t\tvalue >>= 1;\n   873→\t\t\t\tc++;\n   874→\t\t\t}\n   875→\n   876→\t\t\tx[0] ^= direction[0][c];\n   877→\t\t\tx[1] ^= direction[1][c];\n   878→\t\t\tdouble vX = x[0] / SCALE;\n   879→\t\t\tvX *= w;\n   880→\t\t\tvX += xMin;\n   881→\t\t\tdouble vY = x[1] / SCALE;\n   882→\t\t\tvY *= h;\n   883→\t\t\tvY += yMin;\n   884→\t\t\toutput.add(new PVector((float) vX, (float) vY));\n   885→\t\t}\n   886→\n   887→\t\treturn output;\n   888→\t}\n   889→\n   890→\t/**\n   891→\t * Computes the <i>Euclidean minimum spanning tree</i> (EMST) of a set of\n   892→\t * points.\n   893→\t * <p>\n   894→\t * The EMST is a system of line segments, having only the given points as their\n   895→\t * endpoints, whose union includes all of the points in a connected set, and\n   896→\t * which has the minimum possible total length of any such system.\n   897→\t * \n   898→\t * @param points the set of points over which to compute the EMST\n   899→\t * @return a LINES PShape\n   900→\t * @since 1.3.0\n   901→\t */\n   902→\tpublic static PShape minimumSpanningTree(List<PVector> points) {\n   903→\t\t/*\n   904→\t\t * The Euclidean minimum spanning tree in a plane is a subgraph of the Delaunay\n   905→\t\t * triangulation.\n   906→\t\t */\n   907→\t\tIIncrementalTin triangulation = PGS_Triangulation.delaunayTriangulationMesh(points);\n   908→\t\tSimpleGraph<PVector, PEdge> graph = PGS_Triangulation.toGraph(triangulation);\n   909→\t\tSpanningTreeAlgorithm<PEdge> st = new PrimMinimumSpanningTree<>(graph); // faster than kruskal algorithm\n   910→\t\treturn PGS_SegmentSet.toPShape(st.getSpanningTree().getEdges());\n   911→\t}\n   912→\n   913→\t/**\n   914→\t * Computes the hilbert index of a coordinate on a hilbert curve of order n.\n   915→\t */\n   916→\tprivate static int xy2Hilbert(final int px, final int py, final int n) {\n   917→\t\tint i, xi, yi;\n   918→\t\tint s, temp;\n   919→\n   920→\t\tint x = px;\n   921→\t\tint y = py;\n   922→\t\ts = 0; // Initialize.\n   923→\t\tfor (i = n - 1; i >= 0; i--) {\n   924→\t\t\txi = (x >> i) & 1; // Get bit i of x.\n   925→\t\t\tyi = (y >> i) & 1; // Get bit i of y.\n   926→\n   927→\t\t\tif (yi == 0) {\n   928→\t\t\t\ttemp = x; // Swap x and y and,\n   929→\t\t\t\tx = y ^ (-xi); // if xi = 1,\n   930→\t\t\t\ty = temp ^ (-xi); // complement them.\n   931→\t\t\t}\n   932→\t\t\ts = 4 * s + 2 * xi + (xi ^ yi); // Append two bits to s.\n   933→\t\t}\n   934→\t\treturn s;\n   935→\t}\n   936→\n   937→\t/**\n   938→\t * @param values\n   939→\t * @param base\n   940→\t * @param axis         0 = x axis; 1 = y axis\n   941→\t * @param skipZero\n   942→\t * @param truncateBits\n   943→\t */\n   944→\tprivate static void vanDerCorput(float[][] values, int base, int axis, boolean skipZero, int truncateBits) {\n   945→\t\t// https://blog.demofox.org/2017/05/29/when-random-numbers-are-too-random-low-discrepancy-sequences/\n   946→\t\t// https://github.com/Atrix256/SampleZoo/blob/master/src/families/_2d/samples/lds/LDS.cpp\n   947→\t\t// figure out how many bits we are working in.\n   948→\t\tint n = values.length;\n   949→\t\tint value = 1;\n   950→\t\tint numBits = 0;\n   951→\t\twhile (value < n) {\n   952→\t\t\tvalue *= 2;\n   953→\t\t\t++numBits;\n   954→\t\t}\n   955→\t\tint numBitsPreserved = numBits - truncateBits;\n   956→\t\tint bitsPreservedMask = numBitsPreserved > 0 ? Can be negative\n\t * @param n           the number of random points to generate\n\t * @param seed        number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom\n\t *                    number generator\n\t * @return a list of PVector objects representing the (x, y) coordinates of the\n\t *         random points\n\t * @see #ring(double, double, double, double, double, int) non-seeded ring()\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> ring(double centerX, double centerY, double innerRadius, double outerRadius, double maxAngle, int n,\n\t\t\tlong seed) {\n\t\tfinal SplittableRandom random = new SplittableRandom(seed);\n\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n\t\tif (maxAngle == 0) {\n\t\t\tmaxAngle = Double.MIN_VALUE;\n\t\t}\n\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n\t\t\tdouble randomAngle = (maxAngle < 0 ? -1 : 1) * random.nextDouble(Math.abs(maxAngle));\n\t\t\tdouble randomRadius = random.nextDouble(innerRadius, outerRadius);\n\t\t\tdouble x = -Math.sin(randomAngle) * randomRadius;\n\t\t\tdouble y = Math.cos(randomAngle) * randomRadius;\n\n\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector((float) (x + centerX), (float) (y + centerY)));\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn points;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of random points (constrained within a rectangular region)\n\t * via Poisson Disk Sampling.\n\t * <p>\n\t * Poisson-disc sampling produces points that are tightly-packed, but no closer\n\t * to each other than a specified minimum distance, resulting in a more natural\n\t * and desirable pattern for many applications. This distribution is also\n\t * described as blue noise.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin    x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin    y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax    x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax    y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param minDist minimum euclidean distance between any two points\n\t * @return\n\t * @see #poisson(double, double, double, double, double, long) seeded poisson()\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> poisson(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, double minDist) {\n\t\treturn poisson(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, minDist, System.nanoTime());\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of random points (constrained within a rectangular region)\n\t * via Poisson Disk Sampling, using the specified seed.\n\t * <p>\n\t * Poisson-disc sampling produces points that are tightly-packed, but no closer\n\t * to each other than a specified minimum distance, resulting in a more natural\n\t * and desirable pattern for many applications. This distribution is also\n\t * described as blue noise.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin    x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin    y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax    x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax    y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param minDist minimum euclidean distance between any two points\n\t * @param seed    number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n\t *                generator\n\t * @return\n\t * @see #poisson(double, double, double, double, double) non-seeded poisson()\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> poisson(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, double minDist, long seed) {\n\t\tfinal PoissonDistributionJRUS pd = new PoissonDistributionJRUS(seed);\n\t\treturn pd.generate(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, minDist);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a poisson point set having N points constrained within a\n\t * rectangular region.\n\t * <p>\n\t * Poisson-disc sampling produces points that are tightly-packed, but no closer\n\t * to each other than a specified minimum distance, resulting in a more natural\n\t * and desirable pattern for many applications. This distribution is also\n\t * described as blue noise.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param n    target size of poisson point set\n\t * @param seed number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n\t *             generator\n\t * @return\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> poissonN(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n, long seed) {\n\t\tfinal PoissonDistributionJRUS pd = new PoissonDistributionJRUS(seed);\n\t\treturn pd.generate(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, n);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of points arranged in a phyllotaxis pattern (an arrangement\n\t * similar to the florets in the head of a sunflower), using the golden ratio\n\t * (the most irrational number) to position points with the least possible\n\t * aliasing (which is arguably the \"best\" arrangement).\n\t * \n\t * @param centerX x coordinate of the center of the point set\n\t * @param centerY y coordinate of the center of the point set\n\t * @param n       number of points to generate\n\t * @param radius  radius of circular phyllotaxis extent (max distance of a point\n\t *                from the center position)\n\t * @return\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> phyllotaxis(double centerX, double centerY, int n, double radius) {\n\t\treturn phyllotaxis(centerX, centerY, n, radius, 2 * Math.PI - GOLDEN_ANGLE);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of points arranged in a phyllotaxis pattern (an arrangement\n\t * similar to the florets in the head of a sunflower), using a user-defined\n\t * theta.\n\t * \n\t * @param centerX x coordinate of the center of the point set\n\t * @param centerY y coordinate of the center of the point set\n\t * @param n       number of points to generate\n\t * @param radius  radius of circular phyllotaxis extent (max distance of a point\n\t *                from the center position)\n\t * @param theta   angle (in radians) to turn after each point placement\n\t * @return\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> phyllotaxis(double centerX, double centerY, int n, double radius, double theta) {\n\t\tfinal double fillArea = radius * radius * Math.PI; // calculate area to be filled\n\t\tfinal double circleSpace = (fillArea / n); // area per circle\n\t\tfinal double fudge = 0.7; // Fudge factor: breathing space between circles\n\t\tfinal float circleRadius = (float) (Math.sqrt(circleSpace / Math.PI) * fudge);\n\n\t\tfloat cumArea = 0; // cumulative circle area\n\n\t\tfinal List<PVector> outList = new ArrayList<>();\n\t\tfor (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {\n\t\t\tfinal double angle = i * theta; // rotation per circle\n\t\t\tcumArea += circleSpace; // add sm_area to cum_area every loop\n\n\t\t\tfinal double spiralR = Math.sqrt(cumArea / Math.PI); // expansion of spiral (distance of circle) per loop\n\n\t\t\tfloat pX = (float) (centerX + Math.cos(angle) * spiralR); // spiral rotation of golden angle per loop on X\n\t\t\tfloat pY = (float) (centerY + Math.sin(angle) * spiralR); // spiral rotation of golden angle per loop on Y\n\n\t\t\toutList.add(new PVector(pX, pY, circleRadius));\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn outList;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on an irrational number (the\n\t * plastic constant).\n\t * <p>\n\t * The <i>plastic LDS</i> has been <a href=\n\t * \"http://extremelearning.com.au/unreasonable-effectiveness-of-quasirandom-sequences/\">shown</a>\n\t * to have superior low discrepancy properties amongst the quasirandom\n\t * sequences, and is therefore recommended.\n\t * <p>\n\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n\t * @return\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> plasticLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n\t\t// https://github.com/Atrix256/SampleZoo/blob/master/src/families/_2d/samples/irrational_numbers/irrational_numbers.cpp\n\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n\t\tfinal double p = 1.32471795724474602596; // plastic constant\n\t\tfinal double a1 = 1.0 / p; // inverse of plastic number\n\t\tfinal double a2 = 1.0 / (p * p);\n\n\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n\t\t\tfinal float x = (float) (((0.5 + a1 * i) % 1) * w + xMin);\n\t\t\tfinal float y = (float) (((0.5 + a2 * i) % 1) * h + yMin);\n\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x, y));\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn points;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on an irrational number. In this\n\t * method, a random jitter is added to points to give the point set\n\t * blue-noise-like properties.\n\t * <p>\n\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n\t * @return\n\t * @see #plasticJitteredLDS(double, double, double, double, int, long) seeded\n\t *      irrationalJitteredLDS()\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> plasticJitteredLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n\t\treturn plasticJitteredLDS(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, n, System.nanoTime());\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on an irrational number. In this\n\t * method, a random jitter is added to points to give the point set\n\t * blue-noise-like properties.\n\t * <p>\n\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n\t * @param seed number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n\t *             generator\n\t * @return\n\t * @see #plasticJitteredLDS(double, double, double, double, int) non-seeded\n\t *      irrationalJitteredLDS()\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> plasticJitteredLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n, long seed) {\n\t\t// https://github.com/Atrix256/SampleZoo/blob/master/src/families/_2d/samples/irrational_numbers/irrational_numbers.cpp\n\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n\n\t\tfinal SplittableRandom random = new SplittableRandom(seed);\n\t\tfinal double p = 1.32471795724474602596; // plastic constant\n\t\tfinal double a1 = 1.0 / p;\n\t\tfinal double a2 = 1.0 / (p * p);\n\t\tfinal double c_magicNumber = 0.732;\n\n\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n\t\t\tfinal float x = (float) (((random.nextDouble() * c_magicNumber / Math.sqrt(i + 1d) + a1 * i) % 1) * w + xMin);\n\t\t\tfinal float y = (float) (((random.nextDouble() * c_magicNumber / Math.sqrt(i + 1d) + a2 * i) % 1) * h + yMin);\n\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x, y));\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn points;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on a Halton sequence.\n\t * <p>\n\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n\t * @return\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> haltonLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n\t\tfloat[][] values = new float[n][2];\n\t\tvanDerCorput(values, 2, 0, true, 0);\n\t\tvanDerCorput(values, 3, 1, true, 0);\n\n\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n\t\tfor (float[] point : values) {\n\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector((float) (point[0] * w + xMin), (float) (point[1] * h + yMin)));\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn points;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a rectangle)\n\t * from a low discrepancy sequence (LDS) based on a Hammersley sequence.\n\t * <p>\n\t * The Hammersley sequence in 2D is just the 1d Van Der Corput sequence on one\n\t * axis, and regular sampling on the other axis.\n\t * <p>\n\t * Low discrepancy sequences are deterministic (not randomized) number sequences\n\t * that are low discrepancy - meaning the points tend not to clump together and\n\t * leave holes; the resulting point set is more evenly spaced than a simple\n\t * random distribution but less regular than lattices.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n\t * @return\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> hammersleyLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n\n\t\tfloat[][] values = new float[n][2];\n\t\tvanDerCorput(values, 2, 0, false, 0);\n\n\t\tfinal float offset = 1.0f / (n * 2);\n\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n\t\t\tvalues[i][1] = offset + (i / (float) n);\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n\t\tfor (float[] point : values) {\n\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector((float) (point[0] * w + xMin), (float) (point[1] * h + yMin)));\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn points;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of random stratified points (bounded by a rectangle) based on\n\t * the \"N-Rooks\" sampling pattern.\n\t * <p>\n\t * N-Rooks is a sampling pattern where you treat the boundary as if it were a\n\t * chess board. Every sampling position is a rook that could move horizontally\n\t * or vertically, and should be placed such that none of these rooks could\n\t * capture/\"see\" any of the other rooks. In other words, every column has a\n\t * single sample point in it, and every row has a single sample point in it.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n\t * @return\n\t * @see #nRooksLDS(double, double, double, double, int, long)\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> nRooksLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n\t\treturn nRooksLDS(xMin, yMin, xMax, yMax, n, System.nanoTime());\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a set of random stratified points (bounded by a rectangle) based on\n\t * the \"N-Rooks\" sampling pattern.\n\t * <p>\n\t * N-Rooks is a sampling pattern where you treat the boundary as if it were a\n\t * chess board. Every sampling position is a rook that could move horizontally\n\t * or vertically, and should be placed such that none of these rooks could\n\t * capture/\"see\" any of the other rooks. In other words, every column has a\n\t * single sample point in it, and every row has a single sample point in it.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n\t * @param seed number used to initialize the underlying pseudorandom number\n\t *             generator\n\t * @return\n\t * @see #nRooksLDS(double, double, double, double, int)\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> nRooksLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n, long seed) {\n\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n\n\t\tfinal List<Integer> rookPositions = IntStream.range(0, n).boxed().collect(Collectors.toList());\n\t\tCollections.shuffle(rookPositions, new XoRoShiRo128PlusRandom(seed));\n\n\t\tfinal float offset = 1.0f / (n * 2);\n\n\t\tfinal List<PVector> points = new ArrayList<>(n);\n\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {\n\t\t\tfloat x = offset + (rookPositions.get(i) / (float) n);\n\t\t\tx *= w;\n\t\t\tx += xMin;\n\t\t\tfloat y = offset + i / (float) n;\n\t\t\ty *= h;\n\t\t\ty += yMin;\n\t\t\tpoints.add(new PVector(x, y));\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn points;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Generates a 2D set of deterministic stratified points (bounded by a\n\t * rectangle) from the Sobol low discrepancy sequence (LDS).\n\t * <p>\n\t * A Sobol sequence is a low-discrepancy sequence with the property that for all\n\t * values of N,its subsequence (x1, ... xN) has a low discrepancy. It can be\n\t * used to generate pseudo-randompoints in a space S, which are\n\t * equi-distributed.\n\t * \n\t * @param xMin x-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param yMin y-coordinate of boundary minimum\n\t * @param xMax x-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param yMax y-coordinate of boundary maximum\n\t * @param n    number of points to generate\n\t * @since 1.3.1\n\t * @return\n\t */\n\tpublic static List<PVector> sobolLDS(double xMin, double yMin, double xMax, double yMax, int n) {\n\t\tfinal double w = xMax - xMin;\n\t\tfinal double h = yMax - yMin;\n\t\tfinal int dimension = 2;\n\t\tfinal int BITS = 52;\n\t\tfinal double SCALE = FastMath.pow(2, BITS);\n\t\tfinal long[][] direction = new long[dimension][BITS + 1];\n\t\tfinal long[] x = new long[dimension];\n\t\tfinal int[] m = new int[] { 0, 1 };\n\t\tfinal int a = 0;\n\t\tfinal int s = m.length - 1;\n\n\t\tfor (int i = 1; i <= BITS; i++) {\n\t\t\tdirection[0][i] = 1l << (BITS - i);\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// init direction vector\n\t\tfinal int d = 1;\n\t\tfor (int i = 1; i <= s; i++) {\n\t\t\tdirection[d][i] = ((long) m[i]) << (BITS - i);\n\t\t}\n\t\tfor (int i = s + 1; i <= BITS; i++) {\n\t\t\tdirection[d][i] = direction[d][i - s] ^ (direction[d][i - s] >> s);\n\t\t\tfor (int k = 1; k <= s - 1; k++) {\n\t\t\t\tdirection[d][i] ^= ((a >> (s - 1 - k)) & 1) * direction[d][i - k];\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tList<PVector> output = new ArrayList<>(n);\n\t\tfor (int i = 1; i < n; i++) {\n\n\t\t\t// find the index c of the rightmost 0\n\t\t\tint c = 1;\n\t\t\tint value = i - 1;\n\t\t\twhile ((value & 1) == 1) {\n\t\t\t\tvalue >>= 1;\n\t\t\t\tc++;\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tx[0] ^= direction[0][c];\n\t\t\tx[1] ^= direction[1][c];\n\t\t\tdouble vX = x[0] / SCALE;\n\t\t\tvX *= w;\n\t\t\tvX += xMin;\n\t\t\tdouble vY = x[1] / SCALE;\n\t\t\tvY *= h;\n\t\t\tvY += yMin;\n\t\t\toutput.add(new PVector((float) vX, (float) vY));\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn output;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Computes the <i>Euclidean minimum spanning tree</i> (EMST) of a set of\n\t * points.\n\t * <p>\n\t * The EMST is a system of line segments, having only the given points as their\n\t * endpoints, whose union includes all of the points in a connected set, and\n\t * which has the minimum possible total length of any such system.\n\t * \n\t * @param points the set of points over which to compute the EMST\n\t * @return a LINES PShape\n\t * @since 1.3.0\n\t */\n\tpublic static PShape minimumSpanningTree(List<PVector> points) {\n\t\t/*\n\t\t * The Euclidean minimum spanning tree in a plane is a subgraph of the Delaunay\n\t\t * triangulation.\n\t\t */\n\t\tIIncrementalTin triangulation = PGS_Triangulation.delaunayTriangulationMesh(points);\n\t\tSimpleGraph<PVector, PEdge> graph = PGS_Triangulation.toGraph(triangulation);\n\t\tSpanningTreeAlgorithm<PEdge> st = new PrimMinimumSpanningTree<>(graph); // faster than kruskal algorithm\n\t\treturn PGS_SegmentSet.toPShape(st.getSpanningTree().getEdges());\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Computes the hilbert index of a coordinate on a hilbert curve of order n.\n\t */\n\tprivate static int xy2Hilbert(final int px, final int py, final int n) {\n\t\tint i, xi, yi;\n\t\tint s, temp;\n\n\t\tint x = px;\n\t\tint y = py;\n\t\ts = 0; // Initialize.\n\t\tfor (i = n - 1; i >= 0; i--) {\n\t\t\txi = (x >> i) & 1; // Get bit i of x.\n\t\t\tyi = (y >> i) & 1; // Get bit i of y.\n\n\t\t\tif (yi == 0) {\n\t\t\t\ttemp = x; // Swap x and y and,\n\t\t\t\tx = y ^ (-xi); // if xi = 1,\n\t\t\t\ty = temp ^ (-xi); // complement them.\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\ts = 4 * s + 2 * xi + (xi ^ yi); // Append two bits to s.\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn s;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * @param values\n\t * @param base\n\t * @param axis         0 = x axis; 1 = y axis\n\t * @param skipZero\n\t * @param truncateBits\n\t */\n\tprivate static void vanDerCorput(float[][] values, int base, int axis, boolean skipZero, int truncateBits) {\n\t\t// https://blog.demofox.org/2017/05/29/when-random-numbers-are-too-random-low-discrepancy-sequences/\n\t\t// https://github.com/Atrix256/SampleZoo/blob/master/src/families/_2d/samples/lds/LDS.cpp\n\t\t// figure out how many bits we are working in.\n\t\tint n = values.length;\n\t\tint value = 1;\n\t\tint numBits = 0;\n\t\twhile (value < n) {\n\t\t\tvalue *= 2;\n\t\t\t++numBits;\n\t\t}\n\t\tint numBitsPreserved = numBits - truncateBits;\n\t\tint bitsPreservedMask = numBitsPreserved > 0 ? (1 << numBitsPreserved) - 1 : 0;\n\n\t\tfor (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n\t\t\tvalues[i][axis] = 0.0f;\n\t\t\tfloat denominator = base;\n\t\t\tint q = i + (skipZero ? 1 : 0);\n\t\t\tq &= bitsPreservedMask;\n\t\t\twhile (q > 0) {\n\t\t\t\tint multiplier = q % base;\n\t\t\t\tvalues[i][axis] += multiplier / denominator;\n\t\t\t\tq = q / base;\n\t\t\t\tdenominator *= base;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tprivate static class CPVector implements Clusterable {\n\t\tfinal PVector p;\n\t\tfinal double[] point;\n\n\t\tCPVector(PVector p) {\n\t\t\tthis.p = p;\n\t\t\tpoint = new double[] { p.x, p.y };\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t@Override\n\t\tpublic double[] getPoint() {\n\t\t\treturn point;\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n}\n","numLines":988,"startLine":1,"totalLines":988}}}
